ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96006	I thank you, Your Honor, Mr. President of the Croatian Parliament, the Honorable Madam Representatives and gentlemen of Parliament. The Agriculture Act lays down the objectives and measures of the agricultural policy in the Republic of Croatia which are in line with the common EU agricultural policy and the strategic plan of the Ministry of Agriculture and also provides a framework for the implementation of agricultural policy measures. Following the drafting and adoption of new laws on agricultural land and the CAP law and a series of other laws in the last year and a half in the area of agriculture, the adoption of the Agricultural Law constitutes a continuation of a more comprehensive reform of the agricultural policy of the Republic of Croatia. The current Agriculture Act, by unifying a number of national laws, has provided a framework for the establishment of a national agricultural policy and by ensuring the implementation of EU regulations, has created a framework for the functioning of the common agricultural policy. In particular, the implementation of implementing and delegated acts adopted by the EU regulation requires the adoption of a new normative solution which will further regulate both the objectives and measures of agricultural policy and give a clearer framework for their implementation. The new normative solution ensures sustainable development of agriculture, implements the principles of general food safety and the conservation of natural agricultural resources, improves and enhances the competitiveness of the agricultural sector and strengthens the social, social, economic and environmental role of agriculture. In order to ensure consistency in achieving the objectives and implementation of the measures laid down in the last reform of the common agricultural policy, the implementation of 12 UN Regulations which have so far been not an integral part of the applicable law shall be regulated. The responsibility for their implementation is prescribed to the Ministry of Agriculture, the Ministry of Finance, the Ministry of Health and other bodies with which the Ministry of Agriculture may exchange information and conclude cooperation agreements while the paying agency for agriculture, fisheries and rural development is designated as the implementing body of the Agriculture Act, as well as the EU rules relating to measures of agricultural policy. Agriculture is legally highlighted as a strategic branch of the economy in the Republic of Croatia and as such is an integral part of other development strategies. In order to achieve the objectives set, agricultural policy measures and sources of their financing shall be defined. The division of agricultural measures is basically kept in 3 basic categories from the current Agricultural Act and is consistent with the division of measures under the common agricultural policy to measures of the first pillar, direct payments and measures of the common organisation of the market, i.e. the second pillar, etc. rural development. In addition, other measures of national and EU agricultural policies are envisaged. The financing of agricultural policy measures is possible from EU funds intended for rural development and the national budget of the Republic of Croatia, as well as the budget of units of local and regional self-government, as well as donations and other sources in accordance with the applicable legal rules. With the aim of harmonising with the new special regulation in the area of agriculture, the CAP Act provides for a new form of farmers of the self-supply holding. In order to ensure health and consumer protection and to strengthen food trade procedures, a new law introduces international standards for food, guidelines and codes of good practice, etc. alimentarius code. The Ministry of Agriculture is appointed as the national authority of the central and coordination body for tasks related to the code of alimony and assumes the role of national contact point for the code of alimony by the Croatian Institute of Standards. Furthermore, in order to prevent and reduce the production of food waste in all phases of the food chain, the Government of the Republic of Croatia will adopt a plan to prevent and reduce the production of waste from the Republic of Croatia for a period of 4 years and the implementation and detailed development of the measures set out in this plan will be determined by the programme adopted by the Government of the Republic of Croatia on a proposal from the Ministry of Agriculture. In order to prevent food destruction and environmental protection and to help end-users, the food donation system is prescribed. Quality policy of agricultural and food products is increasingly important. The system and quality for agricultural and food products shall also include a protected designation of origin, protected geographical indication and traditional speciality guaranteed as well as mandatory terms, optional quality terms. The proposal for a law prescribes a simplification of the protection of the name of agricultural and food products and a clear procedure is laid down during the registration of the mark at EU level and the time limit for the adoption of protection as well as the time limit for the objection during the national procedure is reduced. A registration obligation shall be laid down for the registration of entities in organic production for legal and natural persons engaged in organic production, processing, distribution, import and export of organic products, and the conditions and procedure for authorising control bodies to carry out controls in organic production shall be governed. The Ministry of Agriculture is responsible for a total of 23 databases. The farmer's register is a central base for farmers in the Republic of Croatia and is run by the paying agency in agriculture, fisheries and rural development. The register of farmers combines data on farmers and their agricultural resources collected through the register of family holdings and the unique register of domestic animals. The data from the farmer's register shall be used to report and control financial support and other measures of agricultural policy and to monitor the situation of agriculture and to plan agricultural policy. The obligation to register for farmers applying for direct support under the agricultural policy measures, farmers applying for market support measures and the rural development measures already mentioned shall be laid down. Where prescribed by sublaws, farmers who are required to be registered in registers, registers or records held by the Ministry or public institutions in agriculture and farmers who sell on the market their own agricultural products produced on the holding on the market. Also, more clearly, procedural rules of entry are laid down in the Register of Farmers. In order to ensure the implementation of the prescribed obligations, administrative inspection, official controls and controls of the Agriculture Payment Agency in its own rural development shall also be defined. The Ministry of Agriculture shall carry out administrative controls and controls on the ground, price collection systems and quantities of certain agricultural food products and certain agricultural instructions for price reporting purposes, the Agency for Payment in Agriculture, Fisheries, Rural Development shall carry out administrative control and control on the ground for all measures and rules for which it is responsible. One of the provisions which continues to be maintained under the current Agricultural Act is a provision which exempts the user from the application of the amount of support of up to 50 000 kuna during one calendar year by means of a measure which remains in force under the current Agricultural Act. Finally, the proposed law improves and simplifies the current framework for the implementation of the common agricultural policy for the 2014-2020 programming financial period, which will ensure more effective implementation of agricultural policy measures and allow for clearer and harmonised handling of competent authorities and ensure transparency in their behaviour. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96067	Thank you, Vice President. Mr. Minister with his associates. So, the 2015 Agriculture Act apparently had a problem with implementation due to non-compliance with the EU acquis. It is precisely this new draft law that regulates the implementation of 12 new EU regulations and ensures the continuation of the implementation of the measures set out in the last reform of so-called ‘EU’. common agricultural policies. In addition to the provisions of the European regulations, this includes the adoption of national strategic and programming documents with national objectives, priorities, measures and mechanisms of action. The new law seeks to improve the legal framework, which in the broadest sense regulates the area of agriculture and agricultural policy. The framework for implementing agricultural policy measures on the provision of state aid in the area of agriculture and rural development, which must be in line with EU rules, is certainly important for continuing to provide support in agriculture. I'm going to say a little bit about these aids, although the share of agricultural policy in the EU budget has been decreasing since the 1980s, still about 31% of the EU's total budget goes to direct payments in agriculture and market measures. 11% will leave for rural development and 16% more funding from the regional fund is intended for rural development goals. Such concentration of funds in rural areas has its own justification. In the vast majority of EU Member States, rural regions are economically lagging behind urban areas, are characterized by a large share of the older population and cover a larger amount of Union territory with significant natural resources. In the upcoming financial period from 2014 to 2020, the proposed objectives of the EU's reformed common agricultural policy include 3 themes, food, the environment and rural areas. For this reason, it is necessary to provide for clearer regulation or more precise regulation of certain provisions of the applicable legislation, in particular the issue of food donation, the provision of support from the state budget, the implementation of measures under the rural development programme, the jurisdiction and the conduct of inspections, and to regulate certain areas that have not been regulated so far in one of the legal regulations. The same applies to implementation... on the application of international standards for food in the Republic of Croatia. So so much has been used, which is 0.3% less than in 2013. Also published data from the State Statistics Office with a state since 1 June 2016. In this respect, the ratio of the population to more than 60 and less than 20 years in rural areas is more unfavourable than in urban areas and other than natural extinction, migration and youth towards cities due to lack of professional opportunities, only makes the demographic, economic and development picture of rural areas worse. Therefore, the proposal for this new Agriculture Act will seek to ensure sustainable development of agriculture, achieve the principles of general food security and the conservation of natural agricultural resources, improve and increase competitiveness, and strengthen the social and social economic and environmental role of agriculture. In fact, the increase in the income of the agricultural producer should be one of the basic objectives of the agricultural policy in the Republic of Croatia, and in this proposal, the law does not even mention it. Furthermore, as regards the procedures laid down in Article 15(2) of the Decision which has been adopted by the paying agency for the users of the measures of this Act and the regulations adopted under this Act are not administrative acts. It should be pointed out here that irregular acts are, in fact, making it difficult or impossible to impose direct, forced implementation of decisions taken because they do not have the property of an executive act. The non-functionality of such a system is more closely demonstrated in procedures e.g. in respect of decisions on the reimbursement of the paying agency's funds. In Article 3 of the same Act, it is necessary to lay down clearly the way in which the decision is to be delivered to users of the measures provided for in this Act and the regulations adopted under this Act by the Agency for Payment in Agriculture, Fisheries and Rural Development and to determine whether the delivery is by electronic means or by mail. So that's one technical thing. Why are we looking at that? So here, maybe we should add that in more detail. Furthermore, we believe that this legislative proposal should certainly encourage the integration or association of small agricultural producers, which would affect their competitiveness, and in this respect we propose a normative regulation of their association obligations in the production processes and distribution of products or other ways to prescribe measures and rules related to the common organisation of the agricultural market. The importance of including local and regional self-government units in setting up agro-centres or agro-incubators should also be highlighted and the importance of clearly determining the needs of manufacturers and strategic planning of these activities. Local communities should organise, establish their own conditions, opportunities and needs, and in some way re-enhancing an active approach and appropriate solutions to restructure, organise and adapt local agricultural production to the needs of time. Ensure sustainable food production, ensure sustainable management of nature and natural resources and promote a balanced development of all rural areas in the EU, as well as in the Republic of Croatia. We have before us a proposal for a Law on Agriculture that regulates the objectives and measures of agricultural policy and provides a framework for the implementation of measures. The law should ensure continued implementation of a set of EU regulations enabling, improving, improving the provision of support to extremely sensitive sectors that are not encouraged in the EU and other state support measures. The common organisation of the markets in agricultural products, the quality system or the registration and protection of the name of agricultural and food products bearing the designation of origin, the geographical indication and the designation of traditional speciality guaranteed. ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96236	Thank you, esteemed Vice President of the Croatian Parliament, respected parliamentary representatives and respected parliamentary representatives. In front of you is the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Consumer Protection Act in the first reading and allow me to propose a draft and present to you in short lines. Two reasons are to prepare or need the preparation of this proposal. First, this bill will bring full compliance with the EU's Directive on unfair business practice. Secondly, the application of the applicable law pointed to the need for improvement and several provisions. So here's to a few short cartoons that we've promoted. Indeed, the applicable consumer protection law for traders is a mandatory rule of emphasis on regular prices and reduced prices during special sales, such as seasonal reductions, action sales or the like. However, the European Commission has drawn attention to the need for a clearer regulation by the Consumer Protection Act that the mandatory emphasis on the regular price and the reduced prices relate exclusively to sales within the business space does not apply to advertising. This would result in full compliance with the provisions of the Unfair Business Practices Directive, removing the possibility of harm and EU law and thus initiating a court case against the Republic of Croatia. This means that the trader can or does not have to highlight the regular and reduced price of advertising, sales outside of business space, distance sales and in cases where... the sales form lasts less than 3 days. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] In order to increase transparency in decision making relating to consumer rights, the provisions of the applicable Consumer Protection Act are also prescribed for the establishment of advisory bodies in public authorities and units of local and regional self-government. These advisory bodies shall decide on the rights and obligations of consumers in particular with regard to the determination of the terms of sale and the prices of certain public services and among the members are representatives of the Consumer Protection Associations. However, the application of these provisions creates difficulties in determining which public authorities have an obligation to set up such advisory bodies. Since such arrangements included an extremely large number of public authorities which do not make decisions in their work in relation to the rights of consumers of public services, it is necessary to define more precisely the bodies which decide on the rights and obligations of consumers of public services. Following this, since the Law on Local and Areaal, the Regional Self-Government has prescribed that consumer protection operations in the self-government area of local self-government units are proposed by this legislative proposal to only implement the establishment of advisory bodies in local self-government units, namely cities and municipalities that decide on the rights and obligations of consumers of public services defined by the Consumer Protection Act. The relevant law also provides for the obligation to be representatives of consumer protection associations in these advisory bodies as well as in the advertising committees of public service providers. In order for consumers, public service users to be better informed, it is proposed that consumer protection organisations publish on their websites a list of units of local self-government and a list of traders, public service providers whose advisory bodies, i.e. commissions, are involved by their representatives. To the same end, it is also proposed that the Croatian Chamber of Commerce and the Croatian Chamber of Commerce publish on their web pages a list of advertising committees involving representatives of consumer protection associations established in these chambers for certain activities carried out by traders with up to 5 employees. Furthermore, in accordance with public procurements conducted by the competent ministry of the Consumer Protection Association, consumers shall carry out project advice from consumers co-financed by State budget funds. Within the framework of consumer consultation projects, 20 thousand advice is provided at year level from different areas such as electronic communications, financial services, public services, contractual relations and the like. This is just saying that consumers themselves have recognised the importance of such counselling. We know that the area of consumer protection is extremely complex and multidisciplinary. In addition, changes in consumer rights at European level have been very common. However, in order to ensure the highest quality of consumer advice, this bill proposes passing exams for consumers from consumer protection organisations carrying out consulting activities under co-financed projects under the state budget. In the e-consultation procedures, we have received proposals from individual associations for consumer protection. A large part of these proposals were adopted, which the proposer felt would contribute to improving the very text of the law. A significant part of the proposal concerns the provisions of the applicable Consumer Protection Act, which are in line with the EU acquis, which the applicant could not adopt because such changes would result in infringements of EU law and thereby initiating proceedings against the Republic of Croatia. However, during 2016 and 2017, the European Commission carried out an analysis of EU legislation in the field of consumer protection resulting in amendments to several applicable rules. In April of this year, a new consumer package of measures was proposed, i.e. a package covering proposal 4 of the existing Directive. The Directive on unfair contractual provisions, the Price Highlighting Directive, the Unfair Commercial Practices Directive and the Consumer Rights Directive. The new consumer package also includes a proposal for a directive on joint suits for the protection of consumer collective interests and repealing Directive No. 22 of 2009 namely, it is a proposal for a directive which will regulate the procedures that authorised bodies for the protection of consumers of Member States will be able to require, in addition to the initiation of the collective consumer protection process, the imposition of measures against traders who have caused a breach of consumer rights by their illegal activities. The measures relate to compensation for damages, repairs, replacements, price reductions, termination of contracts or total repayment of the amount paid. The proposal for a directive will simplify and speed up the compensation procedure in cases of infringement of the rights of a large number of consumers in relation to existing legal legislation, thus raising the level of protection of the rights of all EU consumers and consumers from the territory of the Republic of Croatia. Public dialogue on the topic of the new consumer package, the European Commission is implementing with all Member States and is planning the same in the Republic of Croatia at the end of October this year. I note that the Ministry of Economy, entrepreneurship and crafts, in cooperation with all stakeholders, is constantly working to improve the protection of consumer rights in the Republic of Croatia and thus will continue in the future and all with a view to achieving a high European standard of consumer rights. Following all of this, we propose the adoption of amendments and additions to the Consumer Protection Act and as soon as possible its full application. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96260	Reading the comments from the public consultation, we can see that many associations criticise that both prices, regular and reduced, do not stand out in advertising. However, such an EC opinion is that this definition and compliance with Directive 2005 does not, in fact, substantially alter the existing arrangements but only clearly regulates the exception of the trader's obligation to highlight both the regular price and the price during the duration of the special form of sales. Certainly, until the second reading, especially until the final adoption, we should consider and respect part of the proposal, and the minister himself said that a good part of the proposal has already been agreed, which have a standstill and a foothold in concrete examples of practice. I believe that the proposer will accept what is life and what will help people who meet every day with the potential hidden intentions of someone who offers and sells something to consumers. Furthermore, the law in force requires the establishment of advisory bodies in units of local and regional regional self-government and public authorities which decide on consumer rights and obligations. However, it has been shown in practice that it is necessary to define more precisely which bodies decide on the rights and obligations of consumers of public services. Article 25 of these amendments and additions now stipulates that units of local self-government which decide on the rights and obligations of consumers of public services are obliged to establish an advisory body and make decisions in a transparent, objective and non-discriminatory manner, while traders providing public services are obliged to establish a consumer advertising commission. The Associations will, as we have heard, have their representatives in both bodies and in one body, and it is prescribed that the Association should publish on its websites the list of units of local self-government and the list of traders established by the bodies provided for in this Act. In practice, this means that we who are mayors and mayors will meet with this obligation, for example. when we give and when we give consent to the price list of public services, the collection of mixed municipal waste, biodegradable municipal waste or separate collection of waste. Since the Act on Alternative Dispute Resolution is in force, these amendments stipulate that the consumer can initiate a procedure to resolve disputes against traders before an alternative dispute settlement body while in the current Law this concerned the extrajudicial settlement of consumer disputes before courts of honor and peace centres. We ourselves are aware of the importance of consumer protection associations and we know how much their activities and actions show that they are equal stakeholders in the implementation of consumer protection policy. In relation to consumer protection organisations, it should be stressed that their fundamental role is to fight for a fair market and for social and economic justice for consumers, to help build a consumer protection system, and to care for the voice of consumers at local, regional and global level. The Consumer Protection Act recognises consumer protection associations as an important player in the implementation of consumer protection policy by regulating their activities and actions. The legislator has now made it possible for those persons who are authorised to represent associations to use the activities of these same associations, not to use them for private, business or any other interest. ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96268	Dear Vice President of the Croatian Parliament. Honorable ministers with associates, distinguished ladies and gentlemen, MPs. Today, we have a proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Consumer Protection Act. As regards the establishment of advisory bodies in local self-government units, I believe that this proposal goes towards a further improved consumer protection policy and an active role of the unit of local and regional self-government. It is necessary to strengthen local and regional self-government in the key to implementing this policy, in particular in the implementation of consumer consultation activities, since in practice the implementation of the same problem faced certain difficulties in relation to the extent of its application and in the field of the identification of public authorities which have an obligation to set up advisory bodies. I must also specify that there is no detailed description of the role of the advisory body and no description of who will provide room for advisory bodies and other fees to be received by representatives of the advisory bodies. In order to increase transparency in advisory bodies, as well as in the advertising committees of public service providers, I believe that representatives of consumer protection associations must also be involved, and in order to increase consumer information. These are proposed that consumer protection associations as independent bodies are carried out on their websites a list of units of local self-government and a list of traders whose advisory bodies, i.e. their representatives, participate. As for the role of consumer protection associations, we know that their path and struggle to create a fair market is both social and economic justice for their consumers. However, the practice with the representatives of the Consumer Protection Association has not been in favour of the representatives of the associations or has not provided them with sufficient space for the activities and participation in committees, commercial societies and units of local self-government, where they could take care and be the voice of consumers as independent and impartial bodies. The law also requires the mandatory emphasis on the regular price and the reduction of the price, where it is stressed that the obligation to highlight the two prices refers exclusively to sales within the business premises and does not apply to advertising. But we should take into account and note, how traders have repeatedly complained about the difficulty of mandatory two prices in cases where special sales forms last a shorter period of time. Such a provision has proved to be difficult to implement in practice. For example, when it comes to a special form of sales that starts in a certain part of the day and lasts only a few hours. The obligation to highlight two prices to traders is an obstacle to meeting the legal obligation in question, in particular when there is a large number of products in the trade. In this respect, it is proposed that the obligation to highlight two prices does not apply to situations where special sales forms last for a maximum period of 3 days. This means that each trader in the sales space will be obliged to continue to highlight the regular price and price during a special form of sale, unless such a form of sale takes place within a very short period of time or up to a maximum of 3 days. You also stressed provisions on unfair business practice where a general obligation is prescribed for all traders to conduct their business to consumers fairly. As far as unfair business practices are concerned, it can be both misleading and aggressive with the ability to be passive and active. As an illustration, I will give you some examples of this practice. In the first case, I can say that we have cases where the consumer receives a notice from the NN trader who informs him that he has won a reward of 10 thousand kuna and should take his prize within a certain time limit. However, when the consumer contacts the trader, he receives notice that the notice is only an ad. Instead of rewarding, the consumer gets advice to buy certain products after which he will participate in the prize game. We also have a practice when the consumer bought a certain magazine about photography and left the trader his address to receive a notice of all news about the area, but in the meantime, the consumer starts to get news about other magazines that do not refer to the same things. So that's where we have the same thing, also getting e-mails from bidders where consumers, where their rights have been abolished by consumers. We also need to take account of the scope of these provisions which are limited only to the protection of consumers' economic interests because these provisions do not regulate the issue of other non-economic interests of consumers. Furthermore, other than the need to enable consumers not to receive promotional notices via telephone and messages if they do not want to practice it, they also show that consumers need to be protected when they contract services via telephone. In fact, consumers are very often unaware of the fact that communication with a trader via a telephone related to a particular service has led to a contract being concluded, but this becomes known when the service is provided to them or when an account is issued to them. This proposal to amend the law makes it more clear that the timing of the contract is made by telephone, which I particularly support. In situations where the consumer buys certain products that prove to be incorrect, he often has a dilemma about how in the fastest, easiest and most impoverished way to exercise his rights and get the right thing. News articles on a number of websites and even by authorised persons appear to be confusing information on how to act when the consumer finds that the product he bought is incorrect. There is often also the identification of responsibilities for material disadvantages and guarantees. Although it is indeed legal institutions that have many similarities and often complement situations where both institutions apply can differ significantly. I note how I have noticed when Croatian consumers in their complaints highlight the failure of the product, especially electronic devices, and when objecting to services, they are the most representative of electronic communications. In conclusion, I would like to point out that on behalf of the Party of Labour and Solidarity, reformists and independent representatives, we will support a proposal from the Proposer which aims to further improve consumer protection in the Republic of Croatia, improve the legal framework for the implementation of consumer protection policy activities and policies to monitor the identification of control mechanisms, ensure good business practices and transparency, provide accurate and up-to-date information to consumers. However, I hope that in this debate I have managed to point out that there are also some provisions that need to be complemented by the EU acquis. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96306	Thank you, Mr. Vice President, Mr. Secretary, fellow MPs. I'd say a few things, actually a little bit would come back and say what it's even about protecting consumers, why there's something about the law itself that, as always, is the least talked about here. So, the basic economies, as we know, are contracts, so the contracts that we make every day, we don't even think about it, without which there wouldn't be any trade, there wouldn't be a trade in goods, there wouldn't be a market. Of course, the basis of contract law is the autonomy of party will. So each party willingly enters into a contractual relationship, unless he wants to, he doesn't have to and, of course, has to satisfy certain assumptions. So a person should have a legal and business capacity that is generally acquired by underage, given, means clearly manifesting his will to conclude a contract. And in that sense, of course, contracts are the cornerstone of the market as it exists today. However, on the other hand, there are special contracts in special categories, these are consumer contracts. Which is why they're specific. As counterpart Alexic repeatedly noted, consumer contracts are specific in that we actually have a imbalance between the negotiating position, the parties. So on the one hand, we have a consumer that is by the legal definition of the European regulation, the European rules of the person who concluded the contract and did not, so for some purpose that is not its professional, is not part of its professional activity. And in that sense, of course, that person is not an expert in the area for which the contract is concluded, that person does not have the same negotiating position, usually only has to accept the general conditions offered by other contracting parties such as the bank, simply not sufficiently informed to make a completely autonomous decision and is not at the same level as the other Contracting Party. And for this very reason, it is required of the other party in this case that, for example, banks inform that person properly so that it can manifest its will as it should and indeed make a decision in their own interest. So there's been a lot of talk about these consumer loans, I wouldn't get into it. It also mentioned the European Court of Justice's verdict. I would like to point out this, and I am sorry that there are no colleagues here, because it is important to note that consumer contracts and consumer protection are the EU product. As counterpart Alexic has repeatedly noted, it is the EU that has been since the 1980s since the first directives on the responsibility of manufacturers and sellers for... It is the thing that has developed this consumer law and which, through European law, has entered national legislation through a European aki communitre. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] And there is the EU court, and through this verdict, which was mentioned, played an extremely important role. As regards the judgment itself, it has been adopted which is important to point out in the procedure on the previous issue, meaning when the national court refers, it is referred to that the European Court and the European Court of Justice and the European Court of Justice have taken a decision concerning this particular case. In this particular judgment, the European Court of Justice said, in fact, that C26/13 was, among other things, what counterpart Alexic pointed out that banks in these cases did not adequately inform the other side, that the other parties did not know enough about the risks arising from the currency clause. But what matters is why it is not quite applicable erga omnes and why you cannot automatically deal with all such situations immediately by law. Because you did, and that's what the court said if you look at it, you said not only look at the text of the contract but look at all the information and communication between the bank and the party to determine whether the person was informed in the right way. And in individual cases you can determine, you can determine this, you have to determine this separately for each individual case. So that, of course, we can talk about interpretation here, I can quote to you later on that particular paragraph of that judgment. So it's hard to extrapolate these things in general. So that's one interpretation, I say, judgment can be interpreted in different ways. As far as this one is concerned, that's what I wanted to refer to in view of the whole debate. As far as this law is concerned, which was at least referred to by one essential provision concerning advertising. So in advertising, what the law prescribes and that is the implementation of the European directive is that two prices do not have to be highlighted in the reduction. So you don't have to point out the previous price and the lower price as usual. Now, why is that? It is therefore important to note that contracts can be concluded in an integrated and non-integral text. So the integral text of the contract is when you have one document, you sign it, etc., i.e. both sides immediately sign it, and that's that. However, the contract can also be concluded in non-integral text. So through an offer sent by one party and accepting the offer of the other party. What is important in the context of consumer protection is that public price exposure is considered such an offer. So that's legally important, it's legally important because if the other party accepts the offer, then this side, in this case, is the other party's buyer, then the more seller who made the offer cannot give up that offer. So he's tied to that offer. And that's why it's important for us all to realize that you make a contract every time and when, so every price exposure is actually an offer that you accept and then the employer means you can't sell it at some point because you can't say at some point I'm changing the price after you've already accepted that offer. But what matters is that advertising is not such a case. So advertising is also considered by our Law on Mandatory Relations not only by offering but by calling for acceptance, which means that what it says in the ad does not oblige the person who made the ad. And given this different legal force of trade exposure, there are specific rules for this. And that's actually why this directive provides for specific provisions concerning advertising and why the law then implements it in our legislation. It's about the law, and also what, the second major amendment to the law, it's me already, I've already mentioned it in the replica regarding the Consumer Protection Association. At present, the legal arrangement is such that the minister actually determines which associations belong to, meaning the list which can file collective lawsuits etc.. I personally support the spread of this law, the wider rights of consumer associations should certainly be welcomed. However, what I would draw attention to is that the rules on the establishment of associations that are, and this is the Law on Associations that are properly applied to consumer associations, are very flexible, in terms of very easy to set up an association, you only need three natural persons. And in this sense, here is my suggestion, it might not be a bad thing to increase legal certainty in this area that perhaps consumer associations that have fundamental rights in relation to other associations such as filing complaints, such as having members in advisory bodies, may be subject to specific conditions that those associations may have in relation to the number of members, the length of existence, perhaps the amount of money they have at their expense may be discussed but may be prescribed by law which would be specific conditions that would be higher than those of the general conditions for associations in order to give such associations specific rights. I think that it should be considered and that in this way it would actually increase legal certainty without going again, that the matter would be more transparent, that it would not actually go down in these criteria and allowing everyone to press charges. What fellow Alexic said would the association press charges without having anything to do with it? I mean, it could happen, why? So the association is a legal person. The members of the association are not responsible for the obligations of that association, so if the association submits some kind of lawsuit for who knows what reason, you lose the dispute, then the costs if he loses his salary only at the end of the case and again do not correspond to members of the association, but the association as a legal person. So the one who started the whole thing through the association wouldn't actually be in a position to pay these costs for that reason can be used for due diligence and so I think it's a better way to call this regulation of certain criteria that associations should meet, I think it's a better way than to allow all associations broadly, to anyone who establishes an association, to call a consumer protection association to file collective claims to participate in this whole process that regulates this law, etc. Here is what it would be some of my perhaps suggestions to think about, and of course the law and these amendments of the law we support and they are in line with the directive, but certainly again I am glad that consumer protection and here is being communicated, that this is being talked about in public, and I think that the Republic of Croatia has raised these standards of protection in the last 10 years and I believe that we will continue to go in this direction. ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96312	Thank you, Honored Vice President of the Croatian Parliament, Honored Parliamentary Representatives and Parliamentary Representatives. In front of you is the Final Proposal for a Law on Electronic Issuance in Public Procurement. Therefore, the Ministry of Economy, entrepreneurship and crafts has prepared the Final Proposal for a Law on e-invoicing in public procurement transposing the Directive of the Republic of Croatia into the legal order of the Republic of Croatia. 55 of 2014 hence, the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 which regulates the electronic issue of public procurement accounts. I note that this law is also part of the measures of the national programme for the reform of the Government of Croatia in 2018, and the main objective of the measure, i.e. 100% of the issue and receipt of e-income in public procurement. What exactly is being introduced by this law? This law introduces the obligation to receive e-income in public procurement procedures from 01. 12. In 2018, and the obligation to issue e-indemnity in public procurement procedures since 01. Seventy-seven. 2019. The law will also cover a larger volume of e-accounts, as we have extended the scope to low-value purchase processes, i.e. below 200 thousand kuna for the purchase of goods and services, and the purchase of works below 500 thousand kuna. The law prescribes a central information intermediary for the State i.e. FINA, to which contracting authorities will be obliged to entrust information intermediation services for receiving and sending electronic accounts. However, units of local regional and regional self-government and related companies and sectoral suppliers of the service concerned may entrust any information intermediary registered in the territory of the Republic of Croatia or other EU Member States. What are these benefits, i.e. the advantages that are introduced in business through the application of this law? The cost of processing e-accounts will be significantly lower compared to the cost of processing the traditional paper account. A significant amount of savings will be achieved by digitising the business process from procurement to delivery, shorter account-processing deadlines and faster payments. In electronic invoice procedures for e-income issuers, smaller administrative costs of preparation of print and billing, the cost of postage is eliminated, the account is delivered in a much shorter time and is eventually stored in a secure and controlled electronic archive. The advantage for e-account recipients is the rate of receipt and processing of accounts, which minimises the possibility of human error, ensures faster payment and a high degree of automation of the account exchange process. In the context of this and in the context of the Ministry of Economy, entrepreneurship and crafts' efforts to disburse entrepreneurs or, in the context of unnecessary administrative burden, we consider it necessary to stress that the application of the law will reduce the costs of the private sector's business. According to estimates for the Republic of Croatia, a maximum effect and savings between 6 and 13% of the total amount of public procurement amounting to more than 2.6 billion kuna can be achieved through the establishment of an integrated electronic procurement process. The real fact is that the business sector is more prepared than the state administration and public administration to switch to electronic invoices mostly because many economic operators have used two systems simultaneously, both paper and electronic, because of the lack of adequate legal framework, with real and high costs. It is precisely these costs of this law that will be drastically reduced by its application. As regards the norm to be implemented by this law, I note that there are currently several global and national standards for the electronic issue of invoices that are not interoperable in Member States. Such incompatible legal and technical requirements regarding electronic accounts are often an obstacle to economic operators and prevent them from entering certain new markets. The European electronic account norm does not enter into ways to exchange accounts or prescribe methods and procedures for the same, but only gives guidance on possible models. Tax supervision will be carried out by authorised representatives of the Ministry of Finance, the Tax Administration, while publishers and recipients and their information intermediaries will be responsible for the content of the e-accounts and compliance with the EU-based norm. I would like to add that the European Commission, as an example of others, has published a successful story from Croatia, and has praised the efforts of the Ministry of Economy and Entrepreneurship in promoting e-income in the business of public and state administration bodies. Two implementing rules for this law are also in place. Regulation on technical elements, issue, reception, sending, directing and archiving of electronic accounts and accompanying documents in public procurement and the E-indemnity Compensation Regulation. From all the above, I consider it necessary to adopt this Act and start its application with 1. 12 of this or 1. Seven next year. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96321	We're going to the next item on the agenda. - Proposal for a brand law, first reading, P.Z. No. 417 The proposal is proposed by the Government of the Republic of Croatia. On the basis of Art. 85. Constitution of the Republic of Croatia and Art. 172. ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96322	Respected, the proposed proposal proposes the adoption of a new trade mark law aimed at improving the system of acquisition of this type of intellectual property right as well as improving the right of the holder of the trade mark on the territory of the Republic of Croatia. To this end, the Proposal for a Mark Act is proposed to prescribe understandable and clear rules and effective procedures for the protection of trade marks, which will ensure consistency of conduct and compliance with the legal framework of the European Union and the international legal framework of the field of trade protection. It shall also ensure the complementarity and consistency of the national trade mark protection system with the trade mark protection system at European Union and international level on the so-called framework. Madrid system. Also with regard to the protection of the rights of the holder of the stamps, the aim of the bill is to provide a normative solution that will enable effective protection against the growing appearance of counterfeiting of stamps and thus ensure better protection of consumers from products and services of questionable quality and safety where protected stamps are used unauthorized. The need to adopt a new trade mark law, in particular the fact that a new Directive 2015/246 of the EU, the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 December 2015 on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to trade marks, which is modernising, supplementing, amends the provisions of the previously applicable Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 22 October 2008 on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to trade marks which have so far been harmonised at European Union level with the provisions of the material law on trade marks considered as crucial at the time of its adoption. Directive 2015/246 obliges the EU Member States of the European Union to adopt appropriate laws and regulations by 14 January of the following year in order to comply with the provisions of the Directive. One of the most prominent news adopted by the Directive is the adaptation of the legal framework for the protection of trade marks in the European Union to new technological opportunities... stamps, and the obligation to display a graphic sign so far is lifted. Following this, the stamp will be able to be displayed in any way that allows competent authorities to determine the exact and clear subject of trademark protection, allowing for further development of non-conventional stamps such as sound, mobile and holographic stamps. Furthermore, the Directive establishes an appropriate legal framework for the protection against traditional terms for wines and the traditional speciality of the geographical indication and the designation of origin from the unauthorized registration with a stamp, the introduction of appropriate so-called ‘signature’. the absolute reasons for refusing to register the stamps containing such expressions, and to regulate more detailed rules relating to the registration and protection of joint guarantee stamps. The new law and rules on the labelling and classification of stamps within the meaning of the Regulation on the scope of the protection or products and services to which the trade mark relates in accordance with the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union in Case C370. The novelty is to ensure adequate legal protection for rightholders in relation to counterfeit goods in transit through EU territory and in relation to preparatory actions related to the use or packaging of products not authorised by use of the trade mark. In accordance with the request of Directive 2004/48 on the enforcement of intellectual property rights, the urgency of the proceedings is prescribed for infringement of the trade mark. The subject-matter proposal for a trade mark law with respect to compliance with the obligations to comply with the regulations of the Republic of Croatia with Directive 2015/2436EU shall improve the area of protection of trade marks at national level by setting out clearly the conditions relating to registration, extension of the validity of the trade mark and the availability of a correct mark in order to remove certain concerns that arise in the application of existing legal provisions. Furthermore, the national part of the procedures to be carried out under the international trade mark registration procedure in accordance with the amendments and additions to the relevant international regulation common to the Madrid Agreement of international trade mark registration and the Protocol relating to the Madrid Agreement, which improves the existing rules of international trade mark registration. In view of the extent of the changes envisaged in relation to the existing provisions of the Mark Act, it is proposed to adopt a new law instead of amending and supplementing the existing law adopted in 2003. On five occasions, it was changed and complemented, in line with the relevant guidelines for the regulation. In that respect, the adoption of a new law will facilitate the use of the brand protection system and its compliance with the provisions of its users. First of all, business and business operators will have a particularly favourable impact on the improvement of business environment in the Republic of Croatia. No additional competences of the State Intellectual Property Office or of other State bodies in the registration or protection of marks against infringements shall be exercised by the subject-matter of the Mark Act, nor shall additional financial or other obligations be imposed on trade mark holders. Therefore, the adoption and implementation of this Act will not require the provision of additional resources to the State budget of the Republic of Croatia on top of the funds already being provided for the purpose of regularly carrying out the prescribed activities of existing state bodies. For the purposes of preparing this proposal, a section was established involving representatives of other competent national and judicial authorities participating in the system of enforcement of the right of trade marks. Experts in the field of intellectual property and interested representatives of the social partners and economic associations on the draft law held a public debate through an e-consultation of 30 days in which no objections and proposals were received on the draft law. The draft law was discussed at the 39th session of September 2018 by the Committee on Education, Science and Culture as a parent working body of the Croatian Parliament and the Legislative Committee, and both committees unanimously supported the adoption of this law, with less than a few no-technical remarks to us. Following all of the above, the Croatian Parliament is invited to support the proposed trade mark law in question.

The Madrid system under the auspices of the World Intellectual Property Organisation. The adoption of the Directive has implemented an appropriate reform of the trade mark protection system in the European Union in terms of its modernisation and improved mutual harmonisation of the unitary and national trade mark protection system. Prior to its adoption, it has been established that it is important to exceed the limited part of the coordination achieved by Directive 2008/95 and to extend the harmonisation to other aspects of the material right on trademarks protected by registration under Regulation No. 207/2009. And the principle of effectiveness is particularly important. It means that European law standards are effectively implemented in domestic legislation. ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96333	We move to the next item of the agenda: - Proposal for a law on data and information in health care, first reading, P.Z. Number 422 Proposer is the Government of the Republic of Croatia, pursuant to Article 85. The Constitution of the Republic of Croatia and Article 172. Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament. ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96334	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President. The draft Health Data and Information Act provides the basis for harmonisation of the legal framework for the management of health data and information and their better quality of processing at national health system level and at European Union and international level. In the National Health Development Strategy 12 of 20 years of age, the priority in the strategic developmental direction and measures is the integration and standardisation of health data and information, as well as the harmonisation of the level of information in health. This proposal for a law provides for the implementation of Regulation No 1338 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 December 2008 on Community statistics on public health and health and safety at work. The draft law prescribes standards for improving the quality of information technologies within the framework of eHealth in accordance with national information infrastructure, while reducing the administrative affairs of healthcare professionals, and the Ministry of Health becomes the central body for the eHealth of the Republic of Croatia. The draft law prescribes the rights, obligations and responsibilities of legal and physical persons of the health system of the Republic of Croatia in the field of the management of data and information on health, common principles and standards of quality of processing and use of health data and information, their protection and storage, the catalogue of information standards in the health of the Republic of Croatia, the production of quality public health indicators for public health, management, statistical and scientific research purposes and inspection and professional supervision. The aim of the bill is to make comprehensive and efficient use of health data and information in healthcare to improve and preserve the health of the population in the Republic of Croatia. Thank you very much. I'm at my disposal.

ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96358	Thank you very much, Mr. President. The draft draft law ensures the implementation of Regulation 536 of 2014 on clinical trials of medicinal products for human use and repealing Directive 2001/20 of the European Community which have only partially been achieved by the EU clinical trials. Regulation 536 of 2014 simplified the resolution of requirements for the authorisation of clinical trials by submitting only one dossier with regard to the request of all Member States concerned through the single EU portal. This makes it easier for clinical trial authorities to carry out administrative procedures related to the approval of clinical trials of medicinal products, and Member States cooperate with each other through the portal to assess each individual study. The new approach will contribute to the speed of the process and will stimulate the inclusion of the largest possible number of Member States, thus fostering competitiveness and the implementation of clinical trials in the EU. In the field of clinical trials inspection, further Union controls shall be introduced, with the European Commission preparing in cooperation with Member States the control programme to ensure that Member States monitor compliance with the provisions of the Regulation. The aim of the draft bill is to ensure rapid access to new, innovative treatment procedures while ensuring the safety and protection of respondents through detailed assessment of potential risks and a series of rules adopted by the Regulation. Thank you very much.

This simplifies the resolution of the requirements for the authorisation of a clinical trial by submitting only one dossier with regard to the request of all Member States concerned through a single portal. This makes it easier for clinical trial providers to carry out administrative procedures related to the approval of clinical trials of medicinal products, and Member States cooperate with each other through the portal to assess each individual study. The new approach will also bring about speed of procedures to contribute and stimulate the inclusion of the largest possible number of Member States and thus foster competitiveness and the implementation of clinical studies of medicines in the EU. In the field of clinical trials inspection, further Union controls shall be introduced, with the European Commission preparing itself in cooperation with Member States of the programme, controls to ensure that Member States monitor compliance with the provisions of this Regulation. ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96365	I thank my esteemed colleague, the chairman of the Health Committee, Mrs Ines Strenja-Linic. I'm opening a debate. On behalf of the HDZ Representatives Club, dear colleague Ivan Celic. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96366	I would like to thank Mr Vice-President, the Honorable Secretary of State, first reading of the proposal for a Law implementing Regulation No 536/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on clinical trials of medicinal products for human use and repealing Directive 2001/20 EC. As a clinician with years of experience in clinical studies and now as a member of the Central Ethics Commission, I can say that replacing the 17-year-old directive is not only a harmonisation of Croatia's legislation with EU legislation, but also a better quality of drug studies, as well as greater safety for the subjects themselves who participate in these clinical studies. During the clinical trial, rights, health, dignity and well-being of subjects should be protected, and the data obtained should be reliable and consistent. The interest of the subject should always be preferred over all other interests in order to ensure an independent control of compliance with these principles for clinical trial needs prior authorisation. The concept of clinical study was more specifically defined by the introduction of a broader concept of clinical study with one of its categories of clinical trial. The 2001 Directive aimed at simplifying and harmonising administrative provisions in clinical trials in the Union, however, experience shows that a harmonised approach to the regulation of clinical trials has only been partially achieved. Based on the development of science, it can be concluded that future clinical trials will focus on a population of patients who are more specific, such as subgroups determined by means of... information. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] In order to include a sufficient number of patients for such clinical trials, many or all Member States should be involved. The legal form of the regulation is a priority for test and investigators, so the differences in access between different Member States are reduced to a minimum. The time limits for assessing the documentation regarding the request for approval of clinical trials should be sufficient to assess the documentation and at the same time should ensure rapid access to new, innovative treatment procedures and ensure that the Union remains an attractive place for conducting clinical trials. In the context of this Directive, the concept of silent authorisation was introduced in 2001. This concept should be maintained in order to ensure that deadlines are observed. The Regulation encourages clinical studies to develop medicines for rare diseases and medicinal products intended for subjects suffering from serious and exhausting diseases and often life-threatening, which affects up to 1 in 50 000 people in the Union, and speaks of extremely rare diseases. Member States should assess all requirements for clinical trials effectively and within the specified time limit. The safety risk of subjects in a clinical trial mainly stems from two choices, the drug studied and intervention, however, many clinical trials pose only a minimal additional risk to the safety of subjects compared to normal clinical practice. These low-risk clinical trials are often crucial to assessing the usual treatment and diagnosis that optimises drug use and thus contributes to a high level of public health. These clinical trials should be subject to less strict monitoring rules, requirements for the content of the main file and traceability of the medicinal products examined. In order to improve treatment procedures available to vulnerable groups such as insufferable or older people, people with multiple chronic diseases and people with mental disorders should be fully and adequately studied for the effects of medicinal products that may be of significant clinical value in these specific groups, also as regards requirements relating to their specific characteristics and health protection and welfare of subjects belonging to these groups. The law, though exceptionally, lays down clear rules on informed consent in emergency situations. These situations refer to cases where a patient, for example, he receives a sudden life-threatening disease resulting from multiple injuries, strokes or heart attacks and needs urgent medical intervention. The clinical trial in question should be directly related to a health condition that makes it impossible to obtain prior informed consent from the subject or from his legally appointed representative within the therapeutic window. A large part of the clinical trials are conducted by non-commercial contracting authorities. Non-commercial customers often rely on funding that comes in part or entirely from public funds or charities in order to maximise the valuable contribution of such non-commercial suppliers and to encourage their further research, without jeopardising the quality of clinical trials, it is necessary for Member States to take measures to encourage clinical trials conducted by non-commercial contracting authorities. This Regulation seeks to achieve an internal market in relation to clinical trials and medicinal products for human use and is based on a high level of health protection. This Regulation also lays down high standards of quality and safety of medicinal products in order to meet the normal safety requirements regarding these products, all with a view to ensuring rapid access to new, innovative treatment procedures while at the same time adopting a set of rules on the safety and protection of subjects. Therefore, the HDZ Representatives' Club will support the first reading of the draft Law implementing Regulation No 536/2014. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96368	Thank you. In order to protect the rights of health, dignity and well-being of subjects and to obtain reliable and consistent data during clinical studies of medicinal products, Regulation 536 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on clinical trials of medicinal products for human use and repealing Directive 2001 has been adopted. But I would now like to say something about clinical trials currently in Croatia and the advantages we have been missing for years. So clinical trials are direct investments in Croatia and its health system. Patients are given rapid and direct access to the latest medicines, and doctors are given new scientific knowledge and therapeutic options. Patients participating in clinical trials are given access to the latest innovative medicines that are not normally available on the Croatian market. This is particularly important for cancer patients who are often the last to continue treatment and prolong life and have rare diseases. The benefits and health system because they achieve savings, since the cost of treatment and diagnostic procedures is fully covered by the surveyor. However, the process of approval and negotiation of clinical trials in the Republic of Croatia is too long compared to other European countries. It lasts on average for 8 months, which is twice as long, for example. but in one Hungary. Furthermore, investment in the Republic of Croatia is currently only 9.2 euros per capita for clinical trials. There are currently 200 thousand clinical trials in the world that invests 16 billion euros annually, and it is worrying that 40m euros are invested in the Republic of Croatia annually, and only 9.2 euros per capita, respectively. In Slovenia, for example, It invests 161m euros annually in clinical trials, and in the Republic of Croatia, as we said about 20 million, i.e. in Slovenia as many as 81 euros per capita, in Hungary 158m euros per year, it is 16 euros per capita while in Belgium it invests 2.4 and in Denmark 1.4 billion euros per year. So, compared to the Republic of Croatia, I said wrong a little while ago, 40 million euros. Imagine in one Belgium 2.4 billion euros. According to the Ministry of Health in Croatia, 193 clinical trials are currently being conducted and several are being approved. 69 new clinical trials were approved last year, and in the first few months of this year only 7. From 2009 on average, 65 studies were granted annually. The largest number refers to the study of medicinal products for cancer, chronic inflammatory diseases such as chronic disease, rheumatoid arthritis, cardiovascular disease, multiplesclerosis, diabetes, etc. For example, it is. 61 new clinical trials were agreed in KBC last year. So what is really the importance of clinical trials and how much of this kind of law we needed. Under the current Regulation, in order for a clinical trial of a new medicinal product to begin, it should first be given within 30 days by the Central Ethics Committee on the basis of a very extensive dossier that is required to be provided by the researcher. However, this deadline is often a problem because of the need for further clarifications and documentation as well as the fact that this body meets only once a month. On the basis of a positive opinion, the examination shall then be approved within 30 days by the Ministry of Health. After that, the negotiation process begins with every health institution where the examination is to be conducted, and since there is no deadline for the conclusion of these contracts, the process can be significantly strained. Clinical trials are inevitable because there is no new drug without a previous study, colleague Bulj. For Croatia as a small country, participation in clinical studies is of great importance because they benefit patients who thus get a new treatment option that they would otherwise not otherwise have, as well as the health system and society as a whole is significantly helping our weaker financial opportunities to pay for expensive forms of treatment. It should be noted that we have clinical trials mostly in the 3rd and 4th classes, when the efficacy of this medicinal product has already been demonstrated. So, not when working on healthy volunteers, but when it's already working on patients, which means it's phase 3 and 4. Clinical studies of the medicinal product are therefore carried out in Phase 4. The average is 10 years and the average cost of research is 1.6 billion euros. Medicinal products enter the phase of clinical trials only after there is sufficient firm evidence that the new medicinal product may outweigh the effectiveness of existing medicinal products or be safer to be administered. And that's why this law is important. Therefore, the Republic of Croatia needs to take a clear view and strategy in order to become a country desirable for conducting clinical trials. This includes systematic education of the professional medical public, timely information to patients on clinical trials and the establishment of a national clinical trial registry. Since there are still prejudices in the Republic of Croatia on clinical trials of medicinal products as kind of experiments on humans, the pharmaceutical industry notes that it is strictly controlled processes led by the best healthcare professionals who are daily in contact with patients with the goal of improving outcomes. And therefore the Most Independent Lists will support this Law. Thank you.

Of course, once again, it should be said that the benefits of this Act are not only for Croatian patients who will thus have the possibility of treating the latest medicines but also for investing in this job with clinical studies in Croatia could bring more than 200m euros, since the total amount, as also mentioned by her colleague Strenja-Linić, total global investment related to clinical studies is as much as 16 billion euros. Therefore, 69 clinical studies were approved last year. I believe that the basis of this Law will increase and that the Republic of Croatia will draw closer to the countries in the environment that have a far greater number of clinical studies that thus ensure faster access to innovative therapies for their patients and provide additional income to health systems in their environments. As we have heard, this process has taken too long for us and I hope that this legislative proposal will significantly shorten it and this point is also connected to the previous one, since it is clear that inclusion in clinical studies also requires the availability of quality data and information so that these data and information can be based on clinical trials. Therefore, it is also necessary to provide a quality national clinical trial register here. Therefore, as a club, we support this legislative proposal and we also propose that in transitional provisions that are said to be adopted within 12 months, that the deadline be split and that all legal acts be passed within 6 months. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96371	I thank my esteemed colleague, Zeljko Jovanovic. Next debate, dear colleague Vlatko Kopic, please.
ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96372	Dear colleagues and colleagues, we understand that this legislative proposal is harmonising the legislation of the Republic of Croatia with EU legislation. The existing Regulation sought to simplify and harmonise administrative provisions on clinical trials of medicinal products in the EU, however, experience has shown that a harmonised approach to the regulation of clinical trials has only been partially achieved. Therefore, provisions have been adopted to simplify the procedures for the submission of documentation concerning the application for approval of a clinical trial through a single portal to avoid multiple submission, mainly identical information on a clinical trial and to replace it with the submission of one dossier with documentation relating to the request of all the Member States concerned. Of course I welcome this bill that introduces order in one segment of healthcare which is certainly useful and welcome to the health system of the Republic of Croatia. It is known that clinical trials in EU countries are far more usable, and I am sure that, on the basis of this, all the administrations of our health institutions want to increase the amount of research in their environments. The clinical hospital center Osijek, whose employee I am, is also one of those subjects who is certainly getting the latest innovative medicines from many clinical studies, to get the same preparations in the public health system for free of charge for our patients, and thus we put our health care at an even higher level than we were allowed to do before. I must say that certain financial resources are also received in this procedure which can improve the existing conditions of treatment in which we are working. Thanks to these very valuable and above all professional, competent workers who had enough knowledge and courage to engage in these kinds of jobs, we stand by colleagues and colleagues working on prestigious European and world medical centres. This uniquely uniform way of reporting all clinical studies to be conducted in the future in the EU will certainly facilitate administrative management and start-up, which, in today's conditions, has sometimes slowed down the process of entering the project itself. Since we have some experience with clinical trials so far, I recommend that our resolution ministry here, Secretary of State, consider and analyse the role of each entity in these projects in the coming period, and ultimately make clear recommendations to all participants who are their obligations and rights in such procedures. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2018-10-04-0.u96378	Proposal for a Law implementing EU Regulation No. 536/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on clinical trials in medicinal products for human use and repealing Directive 2001/20/EC, first reading, P.Z.E.
