Any comments and proposals will be sent to the applicant to consider them when establishing the text of the final proposal of the Law. Please, let's vote. I find that most of the 98 votes for and 3 votes have been abstentioned to conclude that it was proposed by the parliamentary working bodies. Dear MPs and MPs, please allow a few words before continuing because on Monday 8 October we are celebrating Independence Day of the Republic of Croatia. 27 years ago, on October 8, 1991, the Croatian Parliament made a unanimous decision to break up the state legal bond between the Republic of Croatia and other republics and provinces of the Socialist Federal Republic of Yugoslavia. In that decision, the Croatian Parliament found that the Republic of Croatia no longer considers any body of the former State legitimate or legal and that it no longer recognises any legal act of any body acting on behalf of the former Federation which no longer exists. This Decision was adopted by the Assembly after the previous day on the basis of the Brijuni Declaration as a quarterly moratorium on the Constitutional Decision on independence and sovereignty adopted by the Assembly on 25 June. A day earlier, JNA aircraft bombed the historic core of Zagreb and the Ban Court in an attempt to kill the Croatian leadership headed by the first Croatian president, Dr. Franjo Tudjman. Due to these circumstances, the historic session of the Parliament was not held in the building of the Croatian Parliament but second place in the INA building in Šubića Street in Zagreb. The decision to break up the state legal bond between the Republics and the provinces of the SFRY was the final point of ending Croatia's deleveraging process from the former state. This decision, together with the Constitutional Decision on independence and sovereignty and the declaration of the declaration of independence and sovereignty of the Republic of Croatia, confirmed the right of the Croatian people to national autonomy, self-determination and the right to a sovereign independent and independent state. The right of the Croatian people to be declared plebiscitarian in a referendum held on 19 May of the same year, wishing to finally achieve a free state based on democracy, the rule of law, multilaterals and the protection of fundamental rights and freedoms. By its decision of 8 October 1991, The Croatian Parliament has once again shown, as has many times during the long centuries of Croatian history, that it is the real holder and guardian of the sovereignty of the Croatian people and of the legal order. Since 8 October 1991 Croatia is starting its path as a sovereign, independent and independent state fighting for international recognition. International recognition of Croatia ended the diplomatic battle for its independence, but due to great Serbian aggression, it was forced to continue fighting for its sovereignty and territorial integrity until 1998 when it returned the Croatian Danube to its constitutional legal order with peaceful reintegration. In defence of modern Croatian statehood, freedom and democracy, many Croatian defenders gave their lives. Therefore, the Homeland War and its values are the cornerstone of the existence of today's Croatia, and our obligation and responsibility are to preserve them and leave them in the legacy of a future generation. Dear MPs and MPs, the Croatian Parliament played a key role in making important decisions to create and defend an independent, independent and democratic Croatia. Today, when we have an independent Croatian state, our responsibility and the only interest must be that all together, in a spirit of mutual respect, we build Croatia as a safe and prosperous country of strong institutions, the rule of law and social security, economically developed and demographically restored with a expressed social sense of justice and solidarity, the protection of family and its values, and the achievement of conditions for strengthening the position of young people. It is our responsibility to respect and listen to the messages of all Croatian citizens through our serious, systematic, courageous and consistent actions in dialogue and tolerance. This year's Independence Day, as you may already know, will be marked on Monday 8 October in St. Mark at 12 o'clock with the last annual ceremony of a large guard shift, and the Croatian Assembly as representative body of Croatian citizens will open their doors from 10 to 17 o'clock. So I invite you all to be here together and celebrate our national holiday. Once again for you and all Croatian citizens and citizens in the country and abroad, as well as for all Croatians and Croats living in Croatia and outside Croatia, I congratulate the Day of Independence of the Republic of Croatia. Thank you.
